Felix no sooner saw the blood than he fainted. He quickly recovered, but he could not endure the sight of the spears, which were removed and hidden from his view. He had seen blood enough spilt at the siege of Iwis, but this came upon him in all its horror unrelieved by the excitement of war.
The two shepherds had been dogged by gipsies, and had been obliged to make a round to escape. They took their revenge by climbing into trees, and as their pursuers passed under thrust them through with their long spears. The shepherds, like all their related tribes, had been at feud with the gipsies for many generations. The gipsies followed them to and from their pastures, cut off stragglers, destroyed or stole their sheep and cattle, and now and then overwhelmed a whole tribe. Of late the contest had become more sanguinary and almost ceaseless.
Mounted on swift, though small, horses, the gipsies had the advantage of the shepherds. On the other hand, the shepherds, being men of great stature and strength, could not be carried away by a rush if they had time to form a circle, as was their custom of battle. They lost many men by the javelins thrown by the gipsies, who rode up to the edge of the circle, cast their darts, and retreated. If the shepherds left their circle they were easily ridden over; while they maintained formation they lost individuals, but saved the mass. Battles were of rare occurrence; the gipsies watched for opportunities and executed raids, the shepherds retaliated, and thus the endless war continued. The shepherds invariably posted sentinels, and sent forward scouts to ascertain if the way were clear. Accustomed to the horrid scenes of war from childhood, they could not understand Felix’s sensitiveness.
They laughed, and then petted him like a spoilt child. This galled him exceedingly; he felt humiliated, and eager to reassert his manhood. He was willing to stay with them there for awhile, nothing would have induced him to leave them now till he had vindicated himself in their sight. The incident happened soon after sunrise, which is very early at the end of June. The camp had only waited for the return of these men, and on their appearance began to move. The march that morning was not a long one, as the sky was clear and the heat soon wearied the flocks. Felix accompanied the scout in advance, armed with his bow, eager to encounter the gipsies.

===Chinese===
去取费利克斯埋藏之物的那两名汉子，直到第二天早晨早餐时分才回来。他们跨进营地，各自的长矛都染着红色，还滴着新鲜的血。费利克斯一见那血，当即晕了过去。他很快苏醒，却受不了见到那两杆矛，人们便把矛拿开，藏到他看不见的地方。围攻伊维斯时，他见过的流血不可谓不多；可这一次，那恐怖全须全尾地压上心头，没有战事的亢奋来稍稍冲淡。
两名牧人被吉普赛人盯了梢，不得不兜了个圈子才得以脱身。他们的报复之法，是爬上树去，等追兵从树下经过，便用长矛把他们捅穿。这些牧人同他们所有亲缘部落一样，与吉普赛人结世仇已历许多代。吉普赛人尾随他们往返于牧场之间，截杀掉队者，毁掉或偷走他们的牛羊，偶尔还吞灭整整一部落。近来，这场争斗愈发血腥，几乎无休无止。
吉普赛人骑着虽小却迅捷的马，占了牧人的上风。另一方面，牧人个个身材魁伟、膂力过人，只要来得及结成圆阵——那是他们惯常的战法——就绝不会被一阵冲杀冲垮。牧人因吉普赛人投掷的投枪折损了不少人——那些人纵马冲到圆阵边缘，掷出标枪，随即退走。牧人若离开圆阵，便轻易会被马队踏过；只要阵形不散，他们损失的是个体，保全的是大众。堂堂之阵的会战并不多见；吉普赛人窥伺时机、发动突袭，牧人则施以报复，于是这场无休止的战争绵延不绝。牧人从不忘记布设哨兵，并派探子在前探明道路是否畅通。他们自幼见惯战争的惨状，无法理解费利克斯的敏感。
他们先是笑，随即又像娇惯一个宠坏的孩子那样抚慰他。这大大刺痛了他；他觉得受了羞辱，急欲重申自己的丈夫气概。他愿意同他们在这里盘桓些时日；此刻任凭什么也休想让他离开他们，除非先在他们眼前重振自己的声威。这件事发生在日出后不久——六月末的日出是非常早的。营地只等这两个人回来，他们一露面，队伍便开拔了。那天早晨的行程不长，因为天空晴朗，暑热很快便使羊群困乏。费利克斯随探子走在前头，手里执着弓，一心盼着撞见吉普赛人。

## XXVI / 二十六

Bow and Arrow / 弓与箭

===Original===
Three mornings the shepherds marched in the same manner, when they came in view of a range of hills so high that to Felix they appeared mountains. The home of the tribe was in these hills, and once there they were comparatively safe from attack. In early spring when the herbage on the downs was scarce, the flocks moved to the meadow-like lands far in the valleys; in summer they returned to the hills; in autumn they went to the vales again. Soon after noon on the third day the scouts reported that a large body of gipsies were moving in a direction which would cut off their course to the hills on the morrow.
The chief held a council, and it was determined that a forced march should be made at once by another route, more to the left, and it was thought that in this way they might reach the base of the slopes by evening. The distance was not great, and could easily have been traversed by the men; the flocks and herds, however, could not be hurried much. A messenger was despatched to the hills for assistance, and the march began. It was a tedious movement. Felix was wearied, and walked in a drowsy state. Towards six o’clock, as he guessed, the trees began to thin, and the column reached the first slopes of the hills. Here about thirty shepherds joined them, a contingent from the nearest camp. It was considered that the danger was now past, and that the gipsies would not attack them on the hill; but it was a mistake.
A large body almost immediately appeared, coming along the slope on the right, not less than two hundred; and from their open movements and numbers it was evident that they intended battle. The flocks and herds were driven hastily into a coombe, or narrow valley, and there left to their fate. All the armed men formed in a circle; the women occupied the centre. Felix took his stand outside the circle by a gnarled and decayed oak. There was just there a slight rise in the ground, which he knew would give him some advantage in discharging his arrows, and would also allow him a clear view. His friends earnestly entreated him to enter the circle, and even sought to bring him within it by force, till he explained to them that he could not shoot if so surrounded, and promised if the gipsies charged to rush inside.
Felix unslung his quiver, and placed it on the ground before him; a second quiver he put beside it; four or five arrows he stuck upright in the sward, so that he could catch hold of them quickly; two arrows he held in his left hand, another he fitted to the string. Thus prepared, he watched the gipsies advance. They came walking their short wiry horses to within half a mile, when they began to trot down the slope; they could not surround the shepherds because of the steep-sided coombe and some brushwood, and could advance only on two fronts. Felix rapidly became so excited that his sight was affected, and his head whirled. His heart beat with such speed that his breath seemed going. His limbs tottered, and he dreaded lest he should faint.

===Chinese===
像这样一连三个早晨，牧人们照原样赶路，随后他们望见一道山岭，高得在费利克斯看来俨然便是群山。部落的家园就在这些山里，一旦到了那里，他们便相对安全，不易遭到攻击。早春时节，丘原上牧草稀薄，羊群便迁往山谷深处那片草场般的土地；夏天，它们回到山上；秋天，又下到谷地里去。第三天午后不久，探子来报：一大队吉普赛人正在移动，其方向恰好截断他们明日进山的去路。
首领开会议事，决定立刻改走另一条路强行军，偏向左方；众人想着这样到傍晚便可抵达山坡脚下。路程并不远，单教男人们赶，本可以轻易走完；然而羊群牛群却催促不得快。一名信使被派往山中求援，行军随即开始。这是一次沉闷的行进。费利克斯疲惫不堪，昏昏沉沉地走着。照他估计，将近六点钟时，树木开始稀疏，队伍抵达了山间最初的坡地。这里约有三十名牧人来同他们会合，是从最近的营地派出的一支人马。众人都以为危险现在已经过去，吉普赛人不会上山来攻击他们了；然而这是失算。
一大队人马几乎立刻出现了，沿着右侧山坡行进而来，不下两百人；从他们毫不掩饰的行动和这样的数目看，他们分明是要寻一战。羊群牛群被急急忙忙赶进一道窄谷（combe）里，任它们在那里听天由命。所有持械的男子围成一个圆圈；妇女们占据中央。费利克斯在圈外一棵虬结朽坏的老橡树旁站定了位置。恰在那里，地面微微隆起，他知道这会让放箭时占到几分便宜，也能让他视野清楚。他的朋友们恳切劝他进圈里去，甚至想用强力把他拉进去，直到他向他们解释，若被这样围在当中，他便无法射击，并许诺吉普赛人一旦冲锋，他就冲进圈里。
费利克斯解下箭壶，放在身前的地上；又把第二只箭壶摆在旁边；四五支箭直插在草皮里，好让他迅即抓到手里；两支箭握在左手中，另一支已扣上弓弦。如此准备停当，他望着吉普赛人逼近。他们骑着短小精悍的健马，走到半英里之内，才开始顺着山坡小跑而下；由于那道两壁陡立的窄谷和几片丛莽，他们无法包围牧人，只能从两个正面推进。费利克斯很快便紧张激动到这般地步：视力受了影响，脑袋直发晕。他的心跳得那样急，气息仿佛就要断绝。四肢摇摇晃晃，他生怕自己会晕倒。

===Original===
His intensely nervous organization, strung up to its highest pitch, shook him in its grasp, and his will was powerless to control it. He felt that he should disgrace himself once more before these rugged but brave shepherds, who betrayed not the slightest symptom of agitation. For one hour of Oliver’s calm courage and utter absence of nervousness he would have given years of his life. His friends in the circle observed his agitation, and renewed their entreaties to him to come inside it. This only was needed to complete his discomfiture. He lost his head altogether; he saw nothing but a confused mass of yellow and red rushing towards him, for each of the gipsies wore a yellow or red scarf, some about the body, some over the shoulder, others round the head. They were now within three hundred yards.
A murmur from the shepherd spearmen. Felix had discharged an arrow. It stuck in the ground about twenty paces from him. He shot again; it flew wild and quivering, and dropped harmlessly. Another murmur; they expressed to each other their contempt for the bow. This immediately restored Felix; he forgot the enemy as an enemy, he forgot himself; he thought only of his skill as an archer, now in question. Pride upheld him. The third arrow he fitted properly to the string, he planted his left foot slightly in advance, and looked steadfastly at the horsemen before he drew his bow.
At a distance of one hundred and fifty yards they had paused, and were widening out so as to advance in loose open rank and allow each man to throw his javelin. They shouted; the spearmen in the circle replied, and levelled their spears. Felix fixed his eye on one of the gipsies who was ordering and marshalling the rest, a chief. He drew the arrow swiftly but quietly, the string hummed, the pliant yew obeyed, and the long arrow shot forward in a steady swift flight like a line of gossamer drawn through the air. It missed the chief, but pierced the horse he rode just in front of the rider’s thigh. The maddened horse reared and fell backwards on his rider.
The spearmen shouted. Before the sound could leave their lips another arrow had sped; a gipsy threw up his arms with a shriek; the arrow had gone through his body. A third, a fourth, a fifth﻿—six gipsies rolled on the sward. Shout upon shout rent the air from the spearmen. Utterly unused to this mode of fighting, the gipsies fell back. Still the fatal arrows pursued them, and ere they were out of range three others fell. Now the rage of battle burned in Felix; his eyes gleamed, his lips were open, his nostrils wide like a horse running a race. He shouted to the spearmen to follow him, and snatching up his quiver ran forward. Gathered together in a group, the gipsy band consulted.

===Chinese===
他那极其敏感的神经组织绷到了顶点，把他牢牢攥住摇晃，他的意志全然无力驾驭它。他觉得，自己又要在这班粗犷而勇敢的牧人面前出丑了，而他们丝毫没有露出慌乱的迹象。只要能换取奥利弗那一个钟头沉着无畏、全无紧张的气度，他情愿付出自己生命中的好几年。圈里的朋友们看出他的慌乱，重新恳求他进到里面去。单这一下，便足以叫他彻底败下阵来。他完全昏了头；眼前只见一团纷乱的黄色和红色朝他扑来，因为吉普赛人个个都披挂黄色或红色的披巾，有的缠在身上，有的斜挂肩头，有的裹在头上。此刻他们已进入三百码之内。
牧人矛手中起了一阵低语。费利克斯已经射出了一箭。箭钉在他面前约二十步的地上。他又射一箭；箭颤巍巍地乱飞，无害地落了下来。又是一阵低语；他们彼此表达着对弓的轻蔑。这话立时使费利克斯恢复了常态；他忘了敌人之作为敌人，忘了他自己；他一心只想着自己身为射手的技艺——它此刻正受人质疑。自尊支撑住了他。第三支箭，他规规矩矩地扣上弦，左脚稍稍在前踏定，凝神注视着那些骑手，然后才拉开弓。
在一百五十码的距离上，他们停住了，正向两翼散开，以便排成疏朗开阔的队列推进，让每个人都掷得出投枪。他们发出呐喊；圈里的矛手们以呐喊回应，放平了长矛。费利克斯盯住一个正指挥调度其余人马的吉普赛人——一个头目。他迅疾而沉稳地引弦开弓，弓弦嗡鸣，柔韧的紫杉应命而屈，长箭平稳迅疾地向前飞去，像一缕抽过空中的游丝。箭没有射中那头目，却射穿了他胯下的马，入箭处恰在骑者大腿之前。那匹发了狂的马人立而起，向后翻倒，压在骑者身上。
矛手们齐声呐喊。喊声未及出口，另一支箭已经飞出；一个吉普赛人惨叫着扬起双臂；箭穿透了他的身体。第三支，第四支，第五支——六个吉普赛人滚倒在草皮上。矛手们的欢呼一声连一声划破长空。全然不惯这种战法的吉普赛人退了下去。致命的箭仍紧追不舍，不等他们退出射程，又有三人倒下。此刻战斗的怒火在费利克斯胸中燃烧；他双眼放光，嘴唇张开，鼻孔大张，像一匹竞赛中的马。他呼喊矛手们跟上，一把抓起箭壶便向前冲。吉普赛人聚作一团，商议起来。

===Original===
Felix ran at full speed; swift of foot, he left the heavy spearmen behind. Alone he approached the horsemen; all the Aquila courage was up within him. He kept the higher ground as he ran, and stopped suddenly on a little knoll or tumulus. His arrow flew, a gipsy fell. Again, and a third. Their anger gave them fresh courage; to be repulsed by one only! Twenty of them started to charge and run him down. The keen arrows flew faster than their horses’ feet. Now the horse and now the man met those sharp points. Six fell; the rest returned. The shepherds came running; Felix ordered them to charge the gipsies. His success gave him authority; they obeyed; and as they charged, he shot nine more arrows; nine more deadly wounds. Suddenly the gipsy band turned and fled into the brushwood on the lower slopes.
Breathless, Felix sat down on the knoll, and the spearmen swarmed around him. Hardly had they begun to speak to him than there was a shout, and they saw a body of shepherds descending the hill. There were three hundred of them; warned by the messenger, the whole country had risen to repel the gipsies. Too late to join in the fight, they had seen the last of it. They examined the field. There were ten dead and six wounded, who were taken prisoners; the rest escaped, though hurt. In many cases the arrow had gone clean through the body. Then, for the first time, they understood the immense power of the yew bow in strong and skilful hands.
Felix was overwhelmed; they almost crushed him with their attentions; the women fell at his feet and kissed them. But the archer could scarcely reply; his intense nervous excitement had left him weak and almost faint; his one idea was to rest. As he walked back to the camp between the chiefs of the shepherd spearmen, his eyes closed, his limbs tottered, and they had to support him. At the camp he threw himself on the sward, under the gnarled oak, and was instantly fast asleep. Immediately the camp was stilled, not to disturb him.

===Chinese===
费利克斯全速奔跑；他脚步轻捷，把沉重的矛手们甩在身后。他独自一人逼近那些骑手；阿奎拉家的全部勇气在他胸中激腾起来。他一路占着高处奔跑，突然收住脚，立在一座小土冈上——那是一座古冢。他的箭飞出，一个吉普赛人倒下。又是一箭，第三个。愤怒给了他们新的勇气：竟被孤身一人击退！二十个人纵马冲来，要将他踏翻。尖利的箭飞得比马蹄还快。忽而马、忽而人，撞上那些锋利的箭镞。六个倒下；其余的退了回去。牧人们奔跑着赶来；费利克斯命令他们向吉普赛人冲锋。他的得胜给了他威权；他们服从了；趁着他们冲锋，他又射出九箭；添了九处致命的创伤。那伙吉普赛人突然转身，逃进下方山坡的丛莽中去了。
费利克斯气喘吁吁，在土冈上坐下，矛手们蜂拥到他周围。他们刚要开口同他说话，便听得一声呐喊——只见一队牧人正下山而来。足有三百人；信使报了信，全境的人都已起来驱逐吉普赛人。他们赶来参战已嫌太迟，只望见了战斗的尾声。他们查看了战场。阵亡的十人，受伤的六人——伤者都被生擒；其余的虽带着伤，却逃掉了。有许多具尸身，箭是整个儿穿透过去的。这时，他们才头一次明白，紫杉弓在强壮而娴熟的手里有着多大的威力。
费利克斯被盛情压倒了；他们的殷勤几乎把他挤坏；妇女们拜倒在他脚下，亲吻他的双足。可这位射手几乎答不上话来；那高度紧张的兴奋过后，他虚弱得几乎要晕倒；他一心只想歇息。他在牧人矛手首领们的簇拥下走回营地时，眼睛闭上了，四肢摇晃起来，他们只得搀扶着他。到了营地，他往那棵虬结老橡树下的草皮上一躺，立时沉沉睡去。营地随即一片寂静，为的是不打搅他。

===Original===
His adventures in the marshes of the buried city, his canoe, his archery, were talked of the livelong night. Next morning the camp set out for their home in the mountains, and he was escorted by nearly four hundred spearmen. They had saved for him the ornaments of the gipsies who had fallen, golden earrings and nose-rings. He gave them to the women, except one, a finger-ring, set with turquoise, and evidently of ancient make, which he kept for Aurora. Two marches brought them to the home of the tribe, where the rest of the spearmen left them. The place was called Wolfstead.
Felix saw at once how easily this spot might be fortified. There was a deep and narrow valley like a groove or green trench opening to the south. At the upper end of the valley rose a hill, not very high, but steep, narrow at the ridge, and steep again on the other side. Over it was a broad, wooded, and beautiful vale; beyond that again the higher mountains. Towards the foot of the narrow ridge here, there was a succession of chalk cliffs, so that to climb up on that side in the face of opposition would be extremely difficult. In the gorge of the enclosed narrow valley a spring rose. The shepherds had formed eight pools, one after the other, water being of great importance to them; and farther down, where the valley opened, there were forty or fifty acres of irrigated meadow. The spring then ran into a considerable brook, across which was the forest.
Felix’s idea was to run a palisade along the margin of the brook, and up both sides of the valley to the ridge. There he would build a fort. The edges of the chalk cliffs he would connect with a palisade or a wall, and so form a complete enclosure. He mentioned his scheme to the shepherds; they did not greatly care for it, as they had always been secure without it, the rugged nature of the country not permitting horsemen to penetrate. But they were so completely under his influence that to please him they set about the work. He had to show them how to make a palisade; they had never seen one, and he made the first part of it himself. At building a wall with loose stones, without mortar, the shepherds were skilful; the wall along the verge of the cliffs was soon up, and so was the fort on the top of the ridge. The fort consisted merely of a circular wall, breast high, with embrasures or crenellations.
When this was finished, Felix had a sense of mastership, for in this fort he felt as if he could rule the whole country. From day to day shepherds came from the more distant parts to see the famous archer, and to admire the enclosure. Though the idea of it had never occurred to them, now they saw it they fully understood its advantages, and two other chiefs began to erect similar forts and palisades.

===Chinese===
他在被埋之城的沼泽里的历险，他的独木舟，他的箭术，被人们谈论了整整一夜。次日清晨，营地的人马启程回山中的家园，将近四百名矛手护卫着他。他们替他保存下阵亡吉普赛人的饰物：金耳环和鼻环。他把这些都分给了妇女们，只除一枚指环——上嵌绿松石，显见是古时的手工——那是他留给奥萝拉的。两天的行程把他们带到部落的家园，其余的矛手在那里同他们分手。这地方名叫沃尔夫斯特德（Wolfstead）。
费利克斯一眼便看出，这地方设防该有多么容易。有一道又深又窄的谷地，像一道刻槽，一条绿色的壕沟，向南敞开。谷地上端耸起一座小山，不算很高，却颇为陡峭，山脊窄窄的，另一面又是陡坡。山那边是一片宽阔、多林而秀美的谷地；再往外，又是更高的群山。在这道窄脊的山脚下，白垩峭壁一段接着一段，因此顶着抵抗从那一面攀爬上来，会极其困难。在这道封闭窄谷的咽喉处，涌出一眼泉水。牧人们在泉下接连修成八口水池——水于他们至关重要；再往下，谷口敞开的地方，有四五十英亩灌溉的草场。泉水随后注入一条相当可观的小溪，溪的对岸便是森林。
费利克斯的主意，是沿小溪边缘筑起一道栅栏，再顺着谷地两侧直筑到山脊。他要在那里造一座堡垒。白垩峭壁的边沿，他要用栅栏或一道墙连接起来，从而合成一个完整的围场。他把这计划说给牧人们听；他们并不怎么热心，因为他们向来不靠这个也安稳无虞——这一带地势崎岖，骑兵根本无从深入。但他们完全处在他影响之下，为讨他欢心，便着手动工了。他只得教他们怎样筑栅栏；他们从未见过那东西，头一段是他亲手做成的。用乱石垒墙、不用灰浆，这门手艺牧人们甚是娴熟；沿峭壁边沿的墙不久便立了起来，山脊顶上的堡垒也随之而成。那堡垒不过是一道圆形的墙，齐胸高，墙上开着射孔，或者叫垛口。
这一切完工之后，费利克斯生出一种主人的感觉，因为在这座堡垒里，他觉得仿佛能统治整个地方。牧人们一天天地从更远的地方前来，看看这位有名的射手，观赏这道围场。这样的念头虽从未进过他们的脑子，如今亲眼见了，便充分明白它的好处，另有两位首领也开始修筑起类似的堡垒和栅栏。

## XXVII / 二十七

Surprised / 遇袭

===Original===
Felix was now anxious to continue his journey, yet he did not like to leave the shepherds, with whom his life was so pleasant. As usual, when deliberating, he wandered about the hills, and then into the forest. The shepherds at first insisted on at least two of their number accompanying him; they were fearful lest the gipsies should seize him, or a Bushman assassinate him. This company was irksome to Felix. In time he convinced them that he was a much better hunter than any of the tribe, and they permitted him to roam alone. During one of these excursions into the forest he discovered a beautiful lake. He looked down on the water from the summit of one of the green mountains.
It was, he thought, half a mile across, and the opposite shore was open woodland, grassy and meadow-like, and dotted with fine old oaks. By degrees these closed together, and the forest succeeded; beyond it again, at a distance of two miles, were green hills. A little clearing only was wanted to make the place fit for a castle and enclosure. Through the grassland opposite he traced the course of a large brook down to the lake; another entered it on the right, and the lake gradually narrowed to a river on his left. Could he erect a tower there, and bring Aurora to it, how happy he would be! A more beautiful spot he had never seen, nor one more suited for every purpose in life.
He followed the course of the stream which left the lake, every now and then disturbing wild goats from the cliffs, and twice he saw deer under the oaks across it. On rounding a spur of down he saw that the river debouched into a much wider lake, which he conjectured must be the Sweet Waters. He went on till he reached the mouth of the river, and had then no doubt that he was standing once more on the shore of the Sweet Water sea. On this, the southern side, the banks were low; on the other, a steep chalky cliff almost overhung the river, and jutted out into the lake, curving somewhat towards him. A fort on that cliff would command the entrance to the river; the cliff was a natural breakwater, so that there was a haven at its base. The river appeared broad and deep enough for navigation, so that vessels could pass from the great Lake to the inland water; about six or seven miles, he supposed.

===Chinese===
费利克斯如今急于继续他的旅程，却又舍不得离开牧人们——与他们同处的日子实在愉快。他惯常如此：每逢斟酌难决之事，便到丘岭间漫游，再走进森林里去。起初牧人们执意要他们之中至少两人陪同他前往；他们生怕吉普赛人掳走他，或是林中人暗算他。这样的陪伴让费利克斯觉得累赘。渐渐地，他说服了他们，让他们相信他比部族里任何人都是好得多的猎手，他们便允许他独自游荡了。在一次深入森林的漫游中，他发现了一座美丽的湖。他从一座青山之巅俯瞰那片水面。
他想，这湖宽约半英里，对岸是开阔的林地，绿草如茵，宛若草甸，点缀着高大的老橡树。渐渐树木合拢，森林接续而来；森林再往外，两英里开外，是青翠的丘陵。只需稍稍清出一方空地，这地方便适宜筑城堡、建围场了。隔着对岸的草地，他辨出一条大溪蜿蜒入湖的走向；另一条从右侧注入湖中；而在他左手，湖面渐渐收窄，化作一条河。倘若他能在那里筑起一座塔楼，把奥萝拉接来，他将是何等幸福！比这更美的地方他从未见过，也没有哪里比这里更宜于人一生的种种用途。
他沿着流出湖去的那条溪水前行，不时惊起崖壁上的野山羊，还两次望见对岸橡树下的鹿。绕过一道丘原的山嘴，他看见这条河注入一片开阔得多的湖面，他猜想那必是甜水海（Sweet Waters）。他一直走到河口，这才确信自己又一次站在了甜水海的岸边。在这一侧，即南岸，堤岸低缓；对岸一道陡峭的白垩峭壁几乎悬临河上，并向湖中突出，微微朝他这边弯过来。在那峭壁上筑一座堡垒，便可扼住河口的入口；峭壁是天然的防波堤，壁脚之下便是一片港湾。这河看来宽深足可行船，船只可以由大湖驶入这片内陆水域；他估计约有六七英里。

===Original===
Felix was much taken with this spot; the beauty of the inland lake, the evident richness of the soil, the river communicating with the great Lake, the cliff commanding its entrance; never, in all his wanderings, had he seen a district so well suited for a settlement and the founding of a city. If he had but a thousand men! How soon he would bring Aurora there, and build a tower, and erect a palisade! So occupied was he with the thought that he returned the whole distance to the spot where he had made the discovery. There he remained a long time, designing it all in his mind.
The tower he would build yonder, three-quarters of a mile, perhaps a mile, inland from the opposite shore, on a green knoll, at the base of which the brook flowed. It would be even more pleasant there than on the shore of the lake. The forest he would clear back a little, and put up a stout palisade, enclosing at least three miles of grassy land. By the shore of the lake he would build his town, so that his vessels might be able to go forth into the great Sweet Water sea. So strongly did imagination hold him that he did not observe how near it was to sunset, nor did he remark the threatening aspect of the sky. Thunder awoke him from his dream; he looked, and saw a storm rapidly coming from the northeast.
He descended the hill, and sheltered himself as well as possible among some thick fir-trees. After the lightning, the rain poured so heavily that it penetrated the branches, and he unstrung his bow and placed the string in his pocket, that it might not become wet. Instantly there was a whoop on either side, and two gipsies darted from the undergrowth towards him. While the terrible bow was bent they had followed him, tracking his footsteps; the moment he unstrung the bow, they rushed out. Felix crushed through between the firs, by main force getting through, but only opening a passage for them to follow. They could easily have thrust their darts through him, but their object was to take him alive, and gratify the revenge of the tribes with torture.

===Chinese===
费利克斯深深迷上了这个地方：内陆湖泊之美，土壤显然肥沃，沟通大湖的河流，扼守河口的峭壁——在他全部的漫游生涯里，从未见过一处地域如此适宜定居、如此适宜建起一座城池。他若有一千个人手该多好！那他该多快就把奥萝拉接来，筑起塔楼，立起栅栏！这个念头如此盘踞他的心，他竟一路折回他发现这地方的原处。他在那里流连了很久，在心中把一切一一规划。
塔楼要建在那边，离对岸往里四分之三英里，也许一英里，在一座青绿的土冈上，溪水就从冈脚流过。那里甚至比湖岸边更为惬意。森林要往后清出些许，再立起一道结实的栅栏，圈进至少三英里的草地。湖岸边则要建起他的市镇，好让他的船只得以驶出，进入浩瀚的甜水海。想象如此紧紧地攫住了他，他竟不曾察觉日头已近西沉，也没有留意天空威胁的面色。一声雷响把他从梦里惊醒；他举目望去，只见一场风暴正从东北方迅速压来。
他下了山，在一片茂密的冷杉间尽量掩蔽自己。闪电过后，大雨倾盆而下，穿透了枝叶，他便卸下弓弦，把弦收进口袋，免得它被打湿。霎时间两边各起一声呼哨，两个吉普赛人从灌木丛中朝他直扑过来。那令人生畏的弓还上着弦时，他们一直缀着他，循着他的脚印追踪；他一卸下弓弦，他们便冲了出来。费利克斯从冷杉之间硬闯，凭蛮力挤出了通路，却也只是为他们开出一条跟来的道。他们本可以轻易把标枪捅穿他的身体，但他们的目的是要活捉他，以酷刑餍足诸部族的复仇。

===Original===
Felix doubled from the firs, and made towards the far-distant camp; but he was faced by three more gipsies. He turned again and made for the steep hill he had descended. With all his strength he raced up it; his lightness of foot carried him in advance, and he reached the summit a hundred yards ahead; but he knew he must be overtaken presently, unless he could hit upon some stratagem. In the instant that he paused to breathe on the summit a thought struck him. Like the wind he raced along the ridge, making for the great Sweet Water, the same path he had followed in the morning. Once on the ridge the five pursuers shouted; they knew they should have him now there were no more hills to breast. It was not so easy as they imagined.
Felix was in splendid training; he kept his lead, and even drew a little on them. Still he knew in time he must succumb, just as the stag, though swifter of foot, ultimately succumbs to the hounds. They would track him till they had him. If only he could gain enough to have time to string and bend his bow! But with all his efforts he could not get away more than the hundred yards, and that was not far enough. It could be traversed in ten seconds, they would have him before he could string it and fit an arrow. If only he had been fresh as in the morning! But he had had a long walk during the day and not much food. He knew that his burst of speed must soon slacken, but he had a stratagem yet.
Keeping along the ridge till he reached the place where the lake narrowed to the river, suddenly he rushed down the hill towards the water. The edge was encumbered with brushwood and fallen trees; he scrambled over and through anyhow; he tore a path through the bushes and plunged in. But his jacket caught in a branch; he had his knife out and cut off the shred of cloth. Then with the bow and knife in one hand he struck out for the opposite shore. His hope was that the gipsies, being horsemen, and passing all their lives on their horses, might not know how to swim. His conjecture was right; they stopped on the brink, and yelled their loudest. When he had passed the middle of the slow stream their rage rose to a shriek, startling a heron far down the water.

===Chinese===
费利克斯从冷杉林里折向一旁，朝远处那座营地奔去；却又迎面撞上三个吉普赛人。他再次转身，奔向他方才下来的那座陡山。他拼尽全力往山上跑；脚下轻快，使他抢在了前头，先对手一百码登上山顶；但他知道，若想不出什么计策，片刻之后必定被追上。他在山顶停步喘息的那一瞬，一个念头闪过心头。他如风一般沿着山脊飞奔，直奔浩瀚的甜水海而去，走的正是他早晨来时的那条路。他一上山脊，五个追兵便高声呼喊起来；他们知道这下定能擒住他了，前面再没有山坡可爬了。事情却没有他们想的那么容易。
费利克斯正锻炼得筋骨极佳；他保住了领先，甚至还稍稍拉开了些距离。但他知道，时间一久他必支撑不住，正如牡鹿虽然脚下更快，终究要败在猎犬手里。他们会一路追踪，直到把他拿到。倘若他能多争出些距离，有工夫上弦弯弓该多好！可是任凭他怎样拼命，领先也无法超过那一百码，而那还不够远。这点距离十秒钟便跑完了，不等他上好弦、搭好箭，他们就会扑到他身上。倘若他像早晨那样精力充沛该多好！可他白天走了长路，又没吃什么东西。他知道这一阵疾驰很快便要松弛下来，但他还有一条计策。
他沿着山脊一直跑到湖面收窄成河的地方，猛然间朝水边直冲下山。岸边堆满了灌木和倒木；他不择路径，翻的翻、钻的钻；他从树丛中撕开一条路，一头扎进水里。可是上衣勾住了一根树枝；他抽出刀，割下那片布条。然后他一手攥着弓和刀，朝对岸奋力游去。他指望的是：吉普赛人身为马上的民族，一辈子都在马背上过活，未必会游泳。他猜对了；他们停在岸边，扯开嗓子吼叫。待他游过这条缓流的中线，他们的怒气涨成一声声尖啸，惊得下游远处水面上一只苍鹭扑翅而起。

===Original===
Felix reached the opposite shore in safety, but the bowstring was now wet and useless. He struck off at once straight across the grasslands, past the oaks he had admired, past the green knoll where in imagination he had built his castle and brought Aurora, through the brook, which he found was larger than it appeared at a distance, and required two or three strokes to cross. A few more paces and the forest sheltered him. Under the trees he rested, and considered what course to pursue. The gipsies would expect him to endeavour to regain his friends, and would watch to cut off his return. Felix determined to make, instead, for another camp farther east, and to get even there by a detour.
Bitterly he reproached himself for his folly in leaving the camp, knowing that gipsies were about, with no other weapon than the bow. The knife at his belt was practically no weapon at all, useful only in the last extremity. Had he a short sword, or javelin, he would have faced the two gipsies who first sprang towards him. Worse than this was the folly of wandering without the least precaution into a territory at that time full of gipsies, who had every reason to desire his capture. If he had used the ordinary precautions of woodcraft, he would have noticed their traces, and he would not have exposed himself in full view on the ridges of the hills, where a man was visible for miles. If he perished through his carelessness, how bitter it would be! To lose Aurora by the merest folly would, indeed, be humiliating.
He braced himself to the journey before him, and set off at a good swinging hunter’s pace, as it is called, that is, a pace rather more than a walk and less than a run, with the limbs somewhat bent, and long springy steps. The forest was in the worst possible condition for movement; the rain had damped the fern and undergrowth, and every branch showered raindrops upon him. It was now past sunset and the dusk was increasing; this he welcomed as hiding him. He travelled on till nearly dawn, and then, turning to the right, swept round, and regained the line of the mountainous hills after sunrise. There he rested, and reached a camp about nine in the morning, having walked altogether since the preceding morning fully fifty miles. This camp was about fifteen miles distant from that of his friends; the shepherds knew him, and one of them started with the news of his safety. In the afternoon ten of his friends came over to see him, and to reproach him.

===Chinese===
费利克斯平安抵达对岸，可弓弦如今已经湿透，派不上用场了。他立刻笔直地横穿草地而去，经过他赞美过的那些橡树，经过那座青绿的土冈——他在想象中曾在那里筑起城堡、接来奥萝拉——蹚过那条溪；这溪比远看时要宽些，须划上两三下才能过去。再走几步，森林便掩蔽了他。他在树下歇息，思量接下来走哪条路。吉普赛人会料到他竭力要回到朋友们那里，必然守候着截断他的归途。费利克斯于是决定改投更东边的另一处营地，而且要绕道过去。
他痛责自己的愚蠢：明知吉普赛人就在四下出没，竟只带一张弓就离开营地。腰间的刀实在算不得武器，只在最后关头才有用处。他若有一柄短剑，或一支投枪，早就迎着最先扑来的那两个吉普赛人拼一场了。比这更蠢的是，竟不带丝毫防备，漫游进一片当时满是吉普赛人的地界——那些人有一切理由巴望捉住他。他若用了林中技艺的寻常防备，便会留意到他们的踪迹，也不至于把自己暴露在众目睽睽的山脊上——在那里，一个人数英里外都望得见。他若因自己的疏忽而丧命，那将是何等苦楚！因这区区蠢行而失去奥萝拉，实在是奇耻大辱。
他振作精神，迎向面前的路程，迈开大步流星的所谓「猎人步」走了起来——那是一种比行走稍快、比奔跑稍慢的步子，四肢微微弯曲，步幅长而富于弹性。森林正处于最不利于行进的状态；雨水打湿了蕨草和林下灌木，每一根枝条都朝他洒下雨珠。这时已过日落，暮色渐浓；他倒欢迎这暮色，因为它掩藏他。他一路走到将近拂晓，然后向右一转，兜了个大圈，日出之后重新回到山岭一线。他在那里歇了歇，约莫早晨九点到达一处营地——自前一日早晨算起，他总共走了足有五十英里。这处营地离他朋友们的营地约十五英里；牧人们认得他，其中一人即刻动身，去报他平安的消息。下午，他的十个朋友过来看他，也来数落他。

===Original===
His weariness was so great that for three days he scarcely moved from the hut, during which time the weather was wet and stormy, as is often the case in summer after a thunderstorm. On the fourth morning it was fine, and Felix, now quite restored to his usual strength, went out with the shepherds. He found some of them engaged in throwing up a heap of stones, flint, and chalk lumps near an oak-tree in a plain at the foot of the hill. They told him that during the thunderstorm two cows and ten sheep had been killed there by lightning, which had scarcely injured the oak.
It was their custom to pile up a heap of stones wherever such an event occurred, to warn others from staying themselves, or allowing their sheep or cattle to stay, near the spot in thunder, as it was observed that where lightning struck once it was sure to strike again, sooner or later. “Then,” said Felix, “you may be sure there is water there!” He knew from his study of the knowledge of the ancients that lightning frequently leaped from trees or buildings to concealed water, but he had no intention of indicating water in that particular spot. He meant the remark in a general sense.
But the shepherds, ever desirous of water, and looking on Felix as a being of a different order to themselves, took his casual observation in its literal sense. They brought their tools and dug, and, as it chanced, found a copious spring. The water gushed forth and formed a streamlet. Upon this the whole tribe gathered, and they saluted Felix as one almost divine. It was in vain that he endeavoured to repel this homage, and to explain the reason of his remark, and that it was only in a general way that he intended it. Facts were too strong for him. They had heard his words, which they considered an inspiration, and *there* was the water. It was no use; *there* was the spring, the very thing they most wanted. Perforce Felix was invested with attributes beyond nature.

===Chinese===
他疲惫至极，三天里几乎不曾离开那间棚屋；这几日天气阴雨多风暴，夏日雷雨之后往往如此。第四天早晨天放晴了，费利克斯的气力也已完全复原，便同牧人们一道出门。他发现有些人正在山脚平原上一棵橡树近旁，堆垒一个石堆，用的是石块、燧石和白垩土块。他们告诉他，雷雨时有两头牛和十只羊在那里被闪电击毙，那棵橡树却几乎无损。
每逢出了这样的事，他们便照惯例堆起一个石堆，警告旁人打雷时不要在近旁逗留，也不要让羊群牛群在那里停留，因为据他们观察，闪电劈过一次的地方，迟早必定再劈。「那么，」费利克斯说，「你们可以断定那底下有水！」他从对古人知识的研究中得知，闪电常从树木或建筑物跃向暗藏的水，可他并无指出那个特定地点有水的意思。他这话本是一般而论。
可是牧人们向来渴求水，又把费利克斯看作与他们不同一等的生灵，便把他随口一句话当了真。他们取来工具挖掘，也是机缘凑巧，竟掘出了一眼丰沛的泉水。水汩汩涌出，汇成一条细流。于是整个部族聚集拢来，向费利克斯行礼，几乎把他当作神明。他竭力推辞这份尊崇，解释他那句话的缘由，说明他本是一般而论，全然无用。事实太硬，压倒了他。他们听见了他的话，认定那是神启，而水*就在那里*。没有法子；*那里*就有泉水，正是他们最想要的东西。费利克斯就这样被强行赋予了超乎自然的禀赋。

===Original===
The report spread; his own old friends came in a crowd to see the new spring, others journeyed from afar. In a week, Felix having meanwhile returned to Wolfstead, his fame had for the second time spread all over the district. Some came a hundred miles to see him. Nothing he could say was listened to; these simple, straightforward people understood nothing but facts, and the defeat of the gipsies and the discovery of the spring seemed to them little less than supernatural. Besides which, in innumerable little ways Felix’s superior knowledge had told upon them. His very manners spoke of high training. His persuasive voice won them. His constructive skill and power of planning, as shown in the palisades and enclosure, showed a grasp of circumstances new to them. This was a man such as they had never before seen.
They began to bring him disputes to settle; he shrank from this position of judge, but it was useless to struggle; they would wait as long as he liked, but his decision they would have, and no other. Next came the sick begging to be cured. Here Felix was firm; he would not attempt to be a physician, and they went away. But, unfortunately, it happened that he let out his knowledge of plants, and back they came. Felix did not know what course to pursue; if by chance he did anyone good, crowds would beset him; if injury resulted, perhaps he would be assassinated. This fear was quite unfounded; he really had not the smallest idea of how high he stood in their estimation.
After much consideration, Felix hit upon a method which would save him from many inconveniences. He announced his intention of forming a herb-garden in which to grow the best kind of herbs, and at the same time said he would not administer any medicine himself, but would tell their own native physicians and nurses all he knew, so that they could use his knowledge. The herb-garden was at once begun in the valley; it could not contain much till next year, and meantime if any diseased persons came Felix saw them, expressed his opinion to the old shepherd who was the doctor of the tribe, and the latter carried out his instructions. Felix did succeed in relieving some small ailments, and thereby added to his reputation.

===Chinese===
风声传扬开来；他的老朋友们成群结队来看新泉，还有人从远方专程赶来。一周之内，费利克斯其间已回到沃尔夫斯特德，他的名声第二次传遍了整个地区。有人从一百英里外来见他。他说什么都没有人听；这些淳朴爽直的人只认事实，击溃吉普赛人与发现泉水这两桩事，在他们看来简直近乎超凡。此外，费利克斯高出众人的见识，还在无数细小处对他们显出了效验。单是他的举止便透着高度的教养。他那富有说服力的嗓音赢得了他们。他在栅栏与围场上显露的营造技艺与谋划之才，显示出一种他们从未见识过的统御情势的本领。这样一个人，他们从来不曾见过。
他们开始把争执带到他面前来裁决；他畏避这裁判者的位置，但挣扎毫无用处；他们愿意等多久都行，可他们要的必是他的裁断，旁人的谁都不要。接着来了乞求治病的病人。这一点费利克斯很坚决；他不愿充作医师，他们便走开了。可是不巧，他无意间露出了自己草木方面的知识，他们便又回来了。费利克斯不知如何是好；倘若他碰巧治好了谁，人群便会围困他；倘若治出了损伤，说不定他会遭暗算。这层忧虑其实全无根据；他实在丝毫想不到，自己在他们心目中的地位有多高。
再三考虑之后，费利克斯想出一个能使他免于许多烦恼的法子。他宣布打算辟一座药草园，种植最上品的药草，同时声明他不亲自施药，而会把所知的一切讲给他们本族的医师和看护们听，好让他们运用这些知识。药草园随即在谷中动工；明年之前园里产不出多少东西，其间若有病人前来，费利克斯便诊看他们，向部族的医生——那位老牧人——陈述自己的见解，后者则照他的指示行事。费利克斯确实缓解了几桩小恙，由此更添声名。

## XXVIII / 二十八

For Aurora / 为奥萝拉

===Original===
Felix now began to find out for himself the ancient truth, that difficulties always confront man. Success only changes them, and increases their number. Difficulties faced him in every direction; at home it had seemed impossible for him to do anything. Now that success seemed to smile on him and he had become a power, instead of everything being smooth and easy, new difficulties sprang up for solution at every point. He wished to continue his journey, but he feared that he would not be permitted to depart. He would have to start away in the night, in which case he could hardly return to them again, and yet he wished to return to these, the first friends he had had, and amongst whom he hoped to found a city.
Another week slipped away, and Felix was meditating his escape, when one afternoon a deputation of ten spearmen arrived from a distant tribe, who had nominated him their king, and sent their principal men to convey the intelligence. Fame is always greatest at a distance, and this tribe in the mountains of the east had actually chosen him as king, and declared that they would obey him whether he took up his residence with them or not. Felix was naturally greatly pleased; how delighted Aurora would be! but he was in perplexity what to do, for he could not tell whether the Wolfstead people would be favourably inclined or would resent his selection.
He had not long to consider. There was an assembly of the tribe, and they, too, chose him by common consent as their king. Secretly they were annoyed that another tribe had been more forward than themselves, and were anxious that Felix should not leave them. Felix declined the honour; in spite of his refusal, he was treated as if he were the most despotic monarch. Four days afterwards two other tribes joined the movement, and sent their acceptance of him as their monarch. Others followed, and so quickly now that a day never passed without another tribe sending a deputation.
Felix thought deeply on the matter. He was, of course, flattered, and ready to accept the dignity, but he was alive to considerations of policy. He resolved that he would not use the title, nor exercise the functions of a king as usually understood. He explained his plan to the chiefs; it was that he should be called simply “Leader,” the Leader of the War; that he should only assume royal authority in time of war; that the present chiefs should retain their authority, and each govern as before, in accordance with ancient custom. He proposed to be king only during wartime. He would, if they liked, write out their laws for them in a book, and so give their customs cohesion and shape. To this plan the tribes readily agreed; it retained all the former customs, it left the chiefs their simple patriarchal authority, and it gave all of them the advantage of combination in war. As the Leader, Felix was henceforth known.

===Chinese===
费利克斯如今开始亲身领悟那条古老的真理：困难总是横在人的面前。成功不过是改换它们的面目，还增添它们的数目。困难曾从四面八方朝他逼来；在家的时候，他似乎什么事也做不成。如今成功仿佛对他绽开笑容，他也成了一方势力，然而一切非但没有变得平顺轻易，新的困难反而在每一处冒出头来，等候解决。他想继续他的旅程，却又怕人家不会放他走。他恐怕只好趁夜动身，而那样一来，他几乎再难回到这些人中间；可他偏偏想回到他们——这些他生平第一批朋友——身边，还指望在他们中间建立起一座城池。
又一个星期悄悄溜走，费利克斯正盘算着怎样脱身，一天下午，一支由十名矛手组成的使团从远方一个部落来到了——那个部落已拥立他为王，特派族中头面人物前来报信。声名总在远处最响亮；东方群山里的这个部落竟真的选了他做王，并且宣称，不论他是否移居到他们那边，他们都甘愿听他号令。费利克斯自然大为欣喜；奥萝拉知道了该多么欢喜啊！可他一时左右为难，不知如何是好，因为他吃不准沃尔夫斯特德的人们对这番拣选是乐见其成，还是心怀怨怼。
他没有多少工夫斟酌。部落开了一场大会，他们同样众口一词，选他做他们的王。暗地里，他们恼恨另一个部落抢在了自己前头，一心只怕费利克斯离开他们。费利克斯辞谢了这份尊荣；尽管他一再推辞，人们待他仍像待一位最专断的君主。四天之后，又有两个部落加入进来，遣使表示奉他为君。接着又有别的部落跟上，如今来得如此之快，没有一天不传来某个部落遣使归附的消息。
费利克斯把这件事思虑得很深。他自然觉得脸上有光，也乐于接受这尊位，但对权谋利害，他心里分明。他决意不用王号，也不行使寻常所理解的君王职权。他向各位首领陈说了自己的方略：他只应称作「统领」，即「战争统领」；唯有战时，他才执掌王权；现任各位首领仍留其权，一如往昔，循古老的习俗各自治理。他提议只在战时为王。若是他们愿意，他还可以把他们的法律为他们写成一册书，使他们的习俗凝然一体、各有形制。诸部落欣然依允这个方略：它保全了全部旧俗，让首领们留住了各自朴素的族长之权，又使众人尽得战时联合之利。从此，费利克斯便以「统领」之名见知于人。

===Original===
In the course of a fortnight, upwards of six thousand men had joined the Confederacy, and Felix wrote down the names of twenty tribes on a sheet of parchment which he took from his chest. A hut had long since been built for him; but he received all the deputations, and held the assemblies which were necessary, in the circular fort. He was so pressed to visit the tribes that he could not refuse to go to the nearest, and thus his journey was again postponed. During this progress from tribal camp to tribal camp, Felix gained the adhesion of twelve more, making a total of thirty-two names of camps, representing about eight thousand spearmen. With pride Felix reflected that he commanded a far larger army than the Prince of Ponze. But he was not happy.
Months had now elapsed since he had parted from Aurora. There were no means of communicating with her. A letter could be conveyed only by a special messenger; he could not get a messenger, and even if one had been forthcoming, he could not instruct him how to reach Thyma Castle. He did not know himself; the country was entirely unexplored. Except that the direction was west, he had no knowledge whatever. He had often inquired of the shepherds, but they were perfectly ignorant. Anker’s Gate was the most westerly of all their settlements, which chiefly extended eastwards. Beyond Anker’s Gate was the trackless forest, of which none but the Bushmen knew anything. They did not understand what he meant by a map; all they could tell him was that the range of mountainous hills continued westerly and southerly for an unascertained distance, and that the country was uninhabited except by wandering gipsy tribes.
South was the sea, the salt water; but they never went down to it, or near it, because there was no sustenance for their flocks and herds. Till now, Felix did not know that he was near the sea; he resolved at once to visit it. As nearly as he could discover, the great fresh water Lake did not reach any farther south; Wolfstead was not far from its southern margin. He concluded, therefore, that the shore of the Lake must run continually westward, and that if he followed it he should ultimately reach the very creek from which he had started in his canoe. How far it was he could not reckon.
There were none of the shepherds who could be sent with a letter; they were not hunters, and were unused to woodcraft; there was not one capable of the journey. Unless he went himself he could not communicate with Aurora. Two routes were open to him; one straight through the forest on foot, the other by water, which latter entailed the construction of another canoe. Journey by water, too, he had found was subject to unforeseen risks. Till he could train some of the younger men to row a galley, he decided not to attempt the voyage. There was but the forest route left, and that he resolved to attempt; but when? And how, without offending his friends?

===Chinese===
半月之间，加入联盟的已逾六千人；费利克斯从他的箱子里取出一幅羊皮纸，在上面写下二十个部落的名字。一座棚屋早已为他盖好；但他接见一切来使、召开一切必要的集会，都在那座圆形堡垒里。众部落再三敦请他亲临，他推辞不过，只得往最近的那几处走了一遭，他的旅程于是又耽搁下来。在这一处营地到那一处营地的巡行途中，费利克斯又收得十二个部落的归附，营地名字合计三十二个，所代表的矛手约八千之数。费利克斯自豪地想到，他麾下的军伍远比庞泽亲王的庞大。可是他并不快乐。
自他与奥萝拉分手，已经过去了几个月。没有任何办法与她互通音问。一封信须得专差才能递到；他寻不到信差，纵然有人肯去，他也指点不出怎样才能走到蒂玛城堡。他自己就不知道路径；这一带乡野全然未经勘探。除了方向在西，他一无所知。他常向牧人们打听，他们却全然茫然。安克门（Anker’s Gate）是他们所有居留地中最西的一处，那些居留地大抵向东延展。安克门之外，便是无路可循的森林，除了林中人，谁也说不出它的一二。他们不懂他说的地图是什么；他们能告诉他的只有：这列山峦丘陵向西、向南绵延，不知几多遥远；那片乡野除游荡的吉普赛诸部落而外，渺无人烟。
南面是海，是咸水；可他们从不下去到海边，也不走近它，因为那里供养不了他们的羊群牛群。直到如今，费利克斯才知道自己离海不远；他当即决意前去一看。就他所能探明的，那浩瀚的淡水大湖再不往南延展；沃尔夫斯特德离大湖的南岸不远。于是他断定，大湖的湖岸必定一路向西绵延不断，只要沿着它走，他终归能抵达自己驾独木舟启程的那条水湾。路程究竟多远，他无从估量。
牧人们里没有一个可以差去送信；他们不是猎手，不谙林中技艺；没有一个担当得起这趟旅程。除非亲自前往，他无法与奥萝拉通问。摆在他面前的路有两条：一条徒步直穿森林，一条取道水路——而水路又须再造一条独木舟。况且他已有过教训，水上之旅常有意料之外的凶险。除非他能训练出一些年轻人来划桨帆船，否则他决意不作此航。剩下的只有森林一路，他决计要走这一条；可是何时动身？又怎样才能不得罪他的朋友们？

===Original===
Meantime, while he revolved the subject in his mind, he visited the river and the shore of the great Lake, this time accompanied by ten spears. The second visit only increased his admiration of the place and his desire to take possession of it. He ascended a tall larch, from whose boughs he had a view out over the Lake; the shore seemed to go almost directly west. There were no islands, and no land in sight; the water was open and clear. Next day he started for the sea; he wished to see it for its own sake, and, secondly, because if he could trace the trend of the shore, he would perhaps be able to put together a mental map of the country, and so assure himself of the right route to pursue when he started for Thyma Castle.
His guides took him directly south, and in three marches (three days) brought him to the strand. This journey was not in a straight line; they considered it was about five-and-thirty or forty miles to the sea, but the country was covered with almost impenetrable forests, which compelled a circuitous path. They had also to avoid a great ridge of hills, and to slip through a pass or river valley, because these hills were frequently traversed by the gipsies who were said, indeed, to travel along them for hundreds of miles. Through the river valley, therefore, which wound between the hills, they approached the sea, so much on a level with it that Felix did not catch a distant glimpse.
In the afternoon of the third day they heard a low murmur, and soon afterwards came out from the forest itself upon a wide bed of shingle, thinly bordered with scattered bushes on the inland side. Climbing over this, Felix saw the green line of the sea rise and extend itself on either hand; in the glory of the scene he forgot his anxieties and his hopes, they fell from him together, leaving the mind alone with itself and love. For the memory of Aurora rendered the beauty before him still more beautiful; love, like the sunshine, threw a glamour over the waves. His old and highest thoughts returned to him in all their strength. He must follow them, he could not help himself. Standing where the foam came nearly to his feet, the resolution to pursue his aspirations took possession of him as strong as the sea. When he turned from it, he said to himself, “This is the first step homewards to her; this is the first step of my renewed labour.” To fulfil his love and his ambition was one and the same thing. He must see her, and then again endeavour with all his abilities to make himself a position which she could share.

===Chinese===
这期间，他一面在心里翻来覆去盘算这件事，一面重访了那条河和大湖的湖岸，这一回有十名矛手随行。第二次到访，只让他对那地方更添赞叹，愈发想把它据为己有。他攀上一株高大的落叶松，从枝柯间望出去，大湖尽收眼底；湖岸几乎是径直向西去的。望不见岛屿，也望不见陆地；水面开阔，澄澈明净。次日他便动身去看海；一来他想单为那海本身看它一眼，二来，倘若他能探明海岸的走向，或许便能凭记忆拼出一幅这一方的地图，好在启程去蒂玛城堡时，确知自己该走的正路。
向导们领他一路向南，三程（三天）之后把他带到了海滨。这趟旅程走的不是直线；他们估量到海边约有三十五或四十英里，但这一路几乎尽是密得穿不透的森林，逼得人只得绕行。他们还须避开一道大山的山脊，从一道隘口或河谷间悄悄溜过去，因为吉普赛人常取道这些山岭往来——据说，他们竟沿着这些山岭一走就是数百英里。于是他们从那条蜿蜒于群山之间的河谷里接近大海；路面与海平面几乎齐平，费利克斯连远远的一瞥也不曾望见。
第三天下午，他们听见一阵低低的轰鸣，不多时便走出了森林本身，来到一片开阔的砾石滩上；滩的向陆一侧，稀稀落落点缀着丛丛灌木。翻过石滩，费利克斯望见大海的绿线冉冉升起，向左右两翼伸展开去；在这一片光景的壮丽之中，他忘却了自己的忧惧与盼望，它们一齐从他身上脱落，只留下这颗心，与它自己、与爱独自相对。因为对奥萝拉的忆念，使眼前的美更见其美；爱，像日光一般，给波浪披上了一层光辉。他昔日那些至高至远的思绪，挟着全部力量回到了他身上。他必须追随它们，由不得自己。他站在浪花几乎涌到脚边的地方，追求抱负的决心攫住了他，强大如这海本身。当他转身离开时，他对自己说：「这是向她回家去的第一步；这是我重振志业的第一步。」成全他的爱与成全他的抱负，原是同一件事。他必须见到她，然后再竭尽自己的全部才智，挣得一个她可以共享的位置。

===Original===
Towards the evening, leaving his escort, he partly ascended the nearest slope of the hills to ascertain more perfectly than was possible at a lower level the direction in which the shore trended. It was nearly east and west, and as the shore of the inland lake ran west, it appeared that between them there was a broad belt of forest. Through this he must pass, and he thought if he continued due west he should cross an imaginary line drawn south from his own home through Thyma Castle; then by turning to the north he should presently reach that settlement. But when he should cross this line, how many days’ travelling it would need to reach it, was a matter of conjecture, and he must be guided by circumstances, the appearance of the country, and his hunter’s instinct.
On the way back to Wolfstead Felix was occupied in considering how he could leave his friends, and yet be able to return to them and resume his position. His general idea was to build a fortified house or castle at the spot which had so pleased him, and to bring Aurora to it. He could then devote himself to increasing and consolidating his rule over these people, and perhaps in time organize a kingdom. But without Aurora the time it would require would be unendurable; by some means he must bring her. The whole day long as he walked he thought and thought, trying to discover some means by which he could accomplish these things; yet the more he considered the more difficult they appeared to him. There seemed no plan that promised success; all he could do would be to risk the attempt.
But two days after returning from the sea it chanced towards the afternoon he fell asleep, and on awakening found his mind full of ideas which he felt sure would succeed if anything would. The question had solved itself during sleep; the mind, like a wearied limb, strained by too much effort, had recovered its elasticity and freshness, and he saw clearly what he ought to do.
He convened an assembly of the chief men of the nearest tribes, and addressed them in the circular fort. He asked them if they could place sufficient confidence in him to assist him in carrying out certain plans, although he should not be able to altogether disclose the object he had in view.

===Chinese===
傍晚时分，他撇下护卫，登上群山最近一道山坡的半腰，好在比低处更确切地辨明海岸的走向。海岸近乎东西横陈；而那内陆湖的湖岸是向西去的，如此看来，两者之间隔着一带宽阔的森林。他必须穿越这片森林；他想，倘若一直向正西走，他终会越过一条想象中的线——一条从他自己的家向南引下、穿过蒂玛城堡的线；那时再折而向北，不久便能抵达那座城堡。可是他何时才越得过这条线、还须走多少天才能到，全然无从悬揣；他只能因势而行——凭乡野的形貌，凭他猎人的直觉。
回沃尔夫斯特德的一路，费利克斯心里盘算的全是：怎样才能离开朋友们，日后却仍能回到他们中间，重掌今日的地位。他大致的图谋是：在那处令他如此倾心的地方筑起一座设防的宅邸或城堡，把奥萝拉接来。此后他便可专心一意，扩植并巩固他对这些人的统治，假以时日，或许还能缔造出一个王国。可是没有奥萝拉，所需的岁月将不堪忍受；无论如何，他必须把她接来。他一路走，一路想，想了整整一天，想寻出一个能成全这一切的法子；然而越是思量，这些事越显得艰难。似乎没有一个计划有望成功；他所能做的，唯有冒险一试。
但从海边归来两天之后，将近午后，他恰巧睡着了，一觉醒来，只觉满心的主意，他确信，只要世上还有能成的事，这些主意便必能成。难题在睡梦中自行解开了；心智如同一根因用力过度而绷乏了的肢体，重又恢复了它的弹性与清新，他清清楚楚地看见了自己该做的事。
他召集附近诸部落的首领人物，开了一场大会，在圆形堡垒里向他们讲话。他问他们，能否给他足够的信任，助他推行某些计划，尽管他心中的意图，他无法和盘托出。

===Original===
They replied as one man that they had perfect confidence in him, and would implicitly obey.
He then said that the first thing he wished was the clearing of the land by the river in order that he might erect a fortified dwelling suitable to his position as their Leader in war. Next he desired their permission to leave them for two months, at the end of which he would return. He could not at that time explain the reasons, but until his journey had been made he could not finally settle among them.
To this announcement they listened in profound silence. It was evident that they disliked him leaving them, yet did not wish to seem distrustful by expressing the feeling.
Thirdly, he continued, he wanted them to clear a path through the forest, commencing at Anker’s Gate and proceeding exactly west. The track to be thirty yards wide in order that the undergrowth might not encroach upon it, and to be carried on straight to the westward until his return. The distance to which this path was cleared he should take as the measure of their loyalty to him.

===Chinese===
众人异口同声地回答：他们对他有完全的信任，唯命是从。
接着他说，他盼望的头一件事，是清出河边的那片土地，好在那里筑起一座设防的居所，与他身为众人战时统领的地位相称。其次，他请求他们准他离开两个月，期满即归。其时他还说不出缘由，但此行不成，他便不能最终在他们中间定居下来。
这番宣告，他们听着，一片深沉的静默。看得出，他们不愿他离去，却又不愿把这层心思说出口，显得自己信不过他。
第三，他接着说，他要他们穿森林开出一条路来，从安克门起，笔直向西。路面须宽三十码，好叫林下灌木无从侵逼；这条路要一直向西开辟下去，直到他归来之日。这条路开到多远，他便拿多远来量度他们对他的忠心。

===Original===
They immediately promised to fulfil this desire, but added that there was no necessity to wait till he left them, it should be commenced the very next morning. To his reiterated request for leave of absence they preserved an ominous silence, and as he had no more to say, the assembly then broke up.
It was afternoon, and Felix, as he watched the departing chiefs, reflected that these men would certainly set a watch upon him to prevent his escape. Without another moment’s delay he entered his hut, and took from their hiding-place the diamond bracelet, the turquoise ring, and other presents for Aurora. He also secured some provisions, and put two spare bowstrings in his pocket. His bow of course he carried.
Telling the people about that he was going to the next settlement, Bedeston, and was anxious to overtake the chief from that place who had attended the assembly, he started. So soon as he knew he could not be seen from the settlement he quitted the trail, and made a wide circuit till he faced westwards. Anker’s Gate was a small outlying post, the most westerly from Wolfstead; he went near it to get a true direction, but not sufficiently near to be observed. This was on the fourth of September. The sun was declining as he finally left the country of his friends, and entered the immense forest which lay between him and Aurora. Not only was there no track, but no one had ever traversed it, unless, indeed, it were Bushmen, who to all intents might be confused with the wild animals which it contained.
Yet his heart rose as he walked rapidly among the oaks; already he saw her, he felt the welcoming touch of her hand; the danger of Bushman or gipsy was nothing. The forest at the commencement consisted chiefly of oaks, trees which do not grow close together, and so permitted of quick walking. Felix pushed on, absorbed in thought. The sun sank; still onward; and as the dusk fell he was still moving rapidly westwards.

===Chinese===
他们当即应承，一定照办，还说不必等他动身，明天一早就开工。可对他一再提出的告假，他们却报以一阵不祥的沉默；他再没有别的话可说，大会便散了。
这时已是午后。费利克斯望着散去的各位首领，心中忖道：这些人必定要派人看守他，防他脱逃。他一刻也不再耽搁，走进自己的棚屋，从藏匿之处取出钻石手镯、绿松石指环，还有其他预备送给奥萝拉的礼物。他又备下了一些干粮，把两根备用弓弦放进口袋。他的弓，当然是随身携带的。
他放出话去，说是要到邻近的居地贝德斯通（Bedeston）去，急着去追赶从那里来、出席过大会的那位首领，随即动身了。一俟确知居地里的人再望不见他，他便离开小径，兜了一个大圈，直到面向正西。安克门是沃尔夫斯特德最西端一处小小的前哨；他走近它，为的是取一个准确的方位，却又不敢近得让人察觉。这一天是九月四日。太阳西斜，他终于离别了朋友们的乡土，走进横亘在他与奥萝拉之间的那片浩瀚森林。那里不但没有路径，而且从不曾有人穿越过——若说有，也唯有林中人；而论其实，林中人与林中栖藏的野兽简直无从分辨。
然而，他在橡树之间疾步而行，心却昂扬起来；他已经望见她了，已经觉出她迎上来的那只手的温存；林中人或吉普赛人的凶险，又何足道哉。森林的起首大半是橡树；这种树不肯挨挤着长，棵棵疏朗，正好快步穿行。费利克斯埋头赶路，全然沉入思绪。太阳沉落了；他仍在前行；暮色四合时，他还在急速地向西而去。
